❯ cavi-key.txt contains a key to another LLM proxy hosted at                    
  http://ai.cavi.au.dk/ - this endpoint advertises a list of available models.  
  Copy this file into a location which can be deployed using build.js but with  
  both the source and destination held in .gitignore. The idea is that I can    
  test against this endpoint locally without exposing or deploying the key. On  
  startup make a JSON request to the expected location of the key, if it is     
  found, replace the existing model panel with a dynamically generated dropdown 
  based on querying this endpoint for its available models, and use it for the  
  AI integration rather than IFM.

● All 1587 tests pass. The three lint errors are existing ES-module parse errors
  in files I didn't touch. Last step: a live end-to-end check against CAVI
  using the real protocol code.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● The tldraw "Ask me anything" panel now uses CAVI instead of IFM when it finds
  the key, and shows a dropdown of CAVI's models. I tested it against the live
  endpoint from node and it returned a real reply. I haven't looked at it in a
  browser, and I haven't run build.js.

  Key file
  - I copied the key into demo/tldraw/cavi-key.json as {"key": "..."}, next to
    index.html, so the page can fetch it as cavi-key.json. build.js copies
    demo/** into docs/, so it will land at docs/demo/tldraw/cavi-key.json.
  - .gitignore now lists cavi-key.txt, demo/tldraw/cavi-key.json and
    docs/demo/tldraw/cavi-key.json, so the committed docs/ tree won't pick up
    the key. I confirmed all three are ignored with git check-ignore.

  demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js
  - fluid.tldraw.ai.localService holds CAVI's settings: the key's location, the
    chat and model-list URLs, and a default model of natai/glm. I picked that
    because natai/glm and cavi/medium both replied quickly in my probes, while
    gpt-oss:20b, qwen3.6:27b and gemma4:31b all timed out at 90 s.
  - findLocalService() fetches the key, then asks CAVI for its models. If the
    key is missing or the model list can't be fetched, it falls back to IFM.
  - The dropdown leaves out speech, transcription and embedding models, which
    leaves 131 of the roughly 150 listed. CAVI marks embedding models as chat,
    so those are filtered out by name.
  - callEndpoint takes an optional endpoint, which defaults to the IFM proxy.

  demo/tldraw/sfc/fluid-demos-askAnything.vue
  - The panel checks for the key on startup. Every send goes through one place
    that picks the endpoint, key and model, so CAVI and IFM share the same
    sending code.
  - With the key present, a dropdown replaces the read-only model box, labelled
    "Model (CAVI)". The password prompt and Log Out button are hidden, and your
    chosen model is remembered in localStorage.
  - If CAVI rejects the key, the error is reported in the conversation instead
    of sending you to the IFM password screen.
  - The password prompt shows briefly on startup until the model list arrives.

  Tests: npm test passes, 1587/1587. I added a test for the model filter and one
  for the whole CAVI path: dropdown, choosing a model, sending, and a rejected
  key. The existing panel test now stubs out the startup check. Lint is clean in
  the files I changed; three files elsewhere in demo/tldraw (tldraw-ai.js,
  tldraw-arena.js, tldraw-clipboard.js) fail to parse because they use
  import/export, and I didn't touch them.

  To change the default model, edit fluid.tldraw.ai.localService.defaultModel.

● Found it. The $compute template the AI wrote never runs: the SFC's own

● I confirmed the bug: the SFC parser overwrites template with a layer object
  that silently replaces the AI's render function, so an empty placeholder <div>
  renders instead. Now testing for other issues once this is fixed.

● Why the Othello widget is a white square

  There are three bugs in it. Only the first is visible today, because it hides
  the second.

  1. The SFC's <template> block silently replaces the layer's template 
  (framework bug, the direct cause). The layer sets view.$component.template to
  a $compute that builds the board as HTML. It also has a <template> block
  holding only <div class="othello-placeholder"></div>. When the SFC is loaded,
  src/framework/core/js/FluidView.js:747 writes template: {sfcLayer: ...} onto
  the same path. fluid.patchDefMap (src/framework/core/js/sfc-parser.js:170-172)
  then replaces whatever value is already there, without any warning. So the
  render function never runs, and the widget shows an empty div. As far as the
  framework is concerned it rendered successfully, so nothing complains: paste
  diagnosis, fluid.tldraw.ai.diagnose and the in-shape problem display all
  report nothing.

  2. {self} refers to the wrong component (AI bug, hidden behind 1). The
  $compute sits on the view subcomponent, so {self}.state.board points at the
  view, which has no state. Once I removed the <template> block, the render
  failed, and the diagnosis said only Rendering of view at path othello-14.view 
  failed: undefined. The template cell had never been computed. It still holds
  the framework's initial placeholder value (fluid.cell.initialUnavailable),
  which carries no message or location.

  3. The starting position is wrong (AI logic bug). The four opening discs are
  at (2,4), (2,5), (3,3) and (3,4). That isn't even a 2×2 square, let alone the
  standard centre (rows and columns 3–4). Separately, it rebuilds the whole
  board as an HTML string and re-parses it on every move instead of using $for.
  That works, but isn't idiomatic.

  To reproduce this under node, I loaded the layer from the PTF file, built the
  widget the way the canvas host does (fluid.tldraw.hostPotentiaFor), rendered
  it and ran the diagnosis. With the <template> block removed and {self} changed
  to {othello}, the board renders and clicking a hint makes a move. The repro
  is in /tmp/claude-1000/-mnt-outer-source-gits-fluid-infusion-6/2a54163c-adbe-4
  c79-ad5b-d126b0811eae/scratchpad/ScratchOthello.js. To run it, copy it and
  ScratchOthello.html back into tests/framework-tests/from-demos/. I didn't
  change any code for this.

  How to surface such errors

  The general problem is that a widget can fail completely while every check
  reports success. The fixes, roughly in order of value:

  A. Fail loudly at the source
  1. Reject a layer that sets both view.template and a <template> block. Make it
     a registration error rather than letting one silently win. On the AI path
     this already becomes "The layer … could not be registered", which starts
     the repair loop. On the paste path it would be reported to the panel.
  2. Give a failure caused by an unresolved reference a real message. At
     minimum, the diagnosis should treat a failure with no message as "never
     computed". It should name the reference that couldn't be resolved
     ({self}.state.board), the component it resolved to (othello.view), and the
     members that do exist there. A message that says {self} is the view and
     suggests {othello} or {$parent} would have let the AI fix bug 2 in one
     round. I haven't yet checked whether the framework deliberately treats a
     missing member as "might appear later"; that decides whether this should be
     an error or a long-running pending state.

  B. Catch "renders nothing" whatever the cause. After the widget settles, flag
  one whose container has no text, no visible children and no images, as
  "renders nothing visible". This simple check would have caught today's case
  even without A1, and it covers mistakes we haven't thought of yet.

  C. Show problems on the widget itself. renderHostProblems in tldraw-driver.js
  currently only replaces the content when construction fails or times out.
  Instead:
  - Run the diagnosis again whenever the widget changes.
  - Show a corner badge ("⚠ 2 problems") that opens up to the details, keeping
    the content visible unless it renders nothing.
  - Add a "Ask the AI to fix this" button. It would hand the problems and the
    widget's PTF to the panel through the existing
    fluid.demos.askAnything.offer.

  D. Feed problems back to the AI.
  - Today, problems found on paste only appear as an error entry in the panel;
    they never reach the AI's conversation. Add a problems part to each widget's
    section of the workspace PTF (workspaceToPTF). The AI would then see broken
    widgets, including ones pasted or broken earlier, on every request.
  - The repair loop on the AI path (maxRepairs) will pick up everything in A and
    B automatically. The Othello reply produced no detectable problems, so it
    was never triggered.
  - Add prompt hints on what {self} means inside a view, not combining
    <template> with view.template, and preferring $for. These are the weakest
    defence, so they should back up the checks rather than replace them.

  I'd start with A1, the "never computed" message from A2, and B. They're small,
  can be tested under node with this widget as the test case, and would have
  turned today's blank square into a repair round. Want me to go ahead with
  those?

      1412              watcher.get();

      1415 +                                                             
      1416 +    /**                                                      
      1417 +     * Explain why a cell's value is unavailable, in terms of
           + the causes which bear messages. A cell may hold an          
      1418 +     * unavailable value without any - for example `fluid.cel
           +l.initialUnavailable`, held by a computed cell which was     
      1419 +     * never evaluated since one of its sources was itself un
           +available for reasons of configuration (see                  
      1420 +     * `fluid.cell.findDirtyEdge`). Such a cell is explained 
           +by searching upstream through the sources of its computed    
      1421 +     * relations for the cells whose unavailable values do be
           +ar messages. The search reads the graph as it stands,        
      1422 +     * without updating or tracking any cell.                
      1423 +     * @param {Cell} cell - The cell whose unavailable value 
           +is to be explained                                           
      1424 +     * @param {Set<Cell>} [visited] - The cells already visit
           +ed by this search                                            
      1425 +     * @return {UnavailableCause[]} The causes found, which i
           +s empty if the cell's value is available or no cause bearing 
      1426 +     * a message could be found                              
      1427 +     */                                                      
      1428 +    fluid.cell.explainUnavailable = function (cell, visited =
           + new Set()) {                                                
      1429 +        const value = cell._value;                           
      1430 +        let togo = [];                                       
      1431 +        if (fluid.isUnavailable(value) && !visited.has(cell))
           + {                                                           
      1432 +            visited.add(cell);                               
      1433 +            const causes = fluid.unavailableToCauses(value).f
           +ilter(cause => cause.message !== undefined);                 
      1434 +            if (causes.length > 0) {                         
      1435 +                togo = causes;                               
      1436 +            } else {                                         
      1437 +                (cell._inEdges || []).forEach(edge => {      
      1438 +                    (edge.sources || []).forEach(source => { 
      1439 +                        fluid.cell.explainUnavailable(source,
           + visited).forEach(cause => {                                 
      1440 +                            if (!togo.includes(cause)) {     
      1441 +                                togo.push(cause);            
      1442 +                            }                                
      1443 +                        });                                  
      1444 +                    });                                      
      1445 +                });                                          
      1446 +            }                                                
      1447 +        }                                                    
      1448 +        return togo;                                         
      1449 +    };                                                       
      1450  };

● I've done all of A–D. Pasting the Othello document now shows the actual causes
  instead of a blank square, and a computed HTML-string template like the AI's
  is fully supported. All 1645 tests pass. I haven't run the tldraw page in a
  browser, so the badge's appearance, the InfusionHost changes and the "Ask the
  AI to fix this" button are untested there. The badge's own logic is tested
  under node.

  What the Othello document now produces
  - As pasted: "Error in SFC for fluid.demos.othello: Its layer defines
    view.$component.template, but the SFC also has a <template> block, which
    would replace it – remove one or the other…"
  - With the <template> block removed: "Member template of component at path
    …view failed: Component at path …view has no member state, although its
    parent component at path … does (at {….view}.state.board)", once for each of
    state, moves and status. These are followed by a single hint explaining
    that {self} inside a $component record means the subcomponent, and
    suggesting {$parent} or a context name instead.
  - Once both are fixed: no problems, and clicking a hint makes a move.

  Framework (src/framework/core/js)
  - The silent "undefined" cause (FluidSignals.js): a computed cell is never
    evaluated while one of its inputs is unavailable for configuration reasons.
    That's deliberate (fluid.cell.findDirtyEdge), but it left the cell holding a
    placeholder with no cause. A new fluid.cell.explainUnavailable(cell) walks
    back through the cell's inputs to the ones that do carry a cause. I didn't
    change the evaluation rules themselves.
  - The {self} mistake (FluidIL.js): fluid.missingMemberUnavailable notes when
    the parent has the missing member, and attaches a new optional hint field to
    the cause (documented in FluidCore.js).
  - The template conflict (FluidView.js): an SFC that sets
    view.$component.template and also has a <template> block is now rejected
    with the message above, instead of the block silently overwriting it.
  - Computed templates (FluidView.js):
    - A template that isn't markup or {sfcLayer} (e.g. a render function that
      returns undefined) gets a clear error instead of a TypeError.
    - Markup is parsed with warnings for unmatched tags, giving the character
      position and a quote. There is also a warning for extra top-level nodes,
      which were silently dropped because only the first is rendered.
    - Text bindings record their source markup, as attribute bindings already
      did.

  Diagnosis (demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js)
  - A failure with no recorded cause is now explained through
    explainUnavailable. Nested causes are flattened, so a throwing render
    function is reported once rather than twice.
  - Members left unavailable by a reference to a missing member are now
    reported. Previously only outright errors were. They're reported at the
    member the user wrote (template) rather than the framework's derived
    templateTree.
  - The rendered template is checked for @{…} bindings that fail to resolve
    (these render as nothing), and for @on… handlers whose method can't be
    resolved or isn't a method. Previously handlers only failed when clicked.
  - A widget that renders but shows nothing (no text, no child elements, no
    images) is flagged with its markup quoted. This catches blank squares from
    causes we haven't anticipated.
  - A conflicting SFC layer part is rejected straight away when the AI sends it,
    rather than as a widget that never becomes available.

  On the widget
  - fluid.tldraw.ai.bindDiagnosis keeps a widget diagnosed as it changes,
    holding back problems until they've lasted 500 ms so transient states during
    construction don't show.
  - InfusionHost shows a "⚠ N problems" badge that expands to list them, with an
    "Ask the AI to fix this" button. That button puts a fix request into the
    panel's input for you to review and send. The badge starts expanded if the
    widget shows nothing else.
  - I also fixed unmount for widgets that failed to construct: they have no
    destroy method, so it now uses fluid.destroyComponent.

  To the AI
  - Each broken widget's entry in the workspace document sent to the AI now
    includes a problems part. If the AI echoes one back, it's ignored.
  - The AI's own apply-and-repair loop already runs the diagnosis on new
    widgets, so it picks all of this up.
  - The prompt now covers: the two ways of supplying a template (never both);
    computed string templates; {self} meaning the view inside the view's
    $compute record but the widget inside the template; and keeping a single
    top-level element.

  Tests: new tests cover the explanation walk and parent hint (FluidILTests.js),
  template warnings and non-string templates (FluidViewTests.js), and the
  conflicting SFC (FluidViewSFCTests.js). TldrawAITests.js adds a compact
  Othello-style board in its conflicting, {self}-broken and fixed forms. It also
  covers the template problems, the empty-widget check, live re-diagnosis, the
  badge, and the problems part. One existing assertion changed because its
  fixture contains an @{nonexistent.member} that was previously not reported.

  I didn't regenerate the .mjs copies in src/framework/core/mjs, since those are
  only rebuilt at releases.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
● I've taken out the fixed delays. A pasted widget's problems are now reported
  as soon as nothing more can happen, using the same fluid.cell.settle() check
  that hydrate.js uses. All 1649 tests pass and the demo is rebuilt, but I
  haven't tried the paste in a browser. Please paste the Othello game again.

  Why you saw nothing: pasting registers the Othello layer, and its SFC is now
  rejected for defining the template twice. The host was waiting for that layer
  to become available, which never happens. So nothing showed until a separate
  8-second fallback timer in the host fired. The badge and the 500 ms delay I
  added last time never came into play, because the widget was never
  constructed.

  What changed:
  - Waiting for a layer: a new fluid.tldraw.ai.awaitLayer (in
    demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js) holds the graph unsettled only while the
    layer is still loading. If the graph settles and the layer still isn't
    available, it returns the reason straight away. The SFC conflict message
    then appears in the shape immediately, and the paste report goes to the
    panel.
  - A widget that never becomes available (for example, the {self} variant):
    once the graph settles, it is described in the shape and reported to whoever
    is waiting on it, instead of after 8 seconds.
  - The badge: it now updates each time the graph settles, instead of after the
    500 ms debounce.
  - The AI's apply-and-repair loop (demo/tldraw/js/tldraw-ai.js): the fixed 500
    ms pause before checking new widgets is replaced by waiting for the graph to
    settle. The same applies when parts are applied to an existing widget.

  An 8-second cap remains only as a fallback, for work that never finishes, such
  as a network request that hangs; anything still waiting holds the graph
  unsettled. Hitting it should now be rare.

  Tests: I added tests in tests/framework-tests/from-demos/js/TldrawAITests.js.
  A rejected SFC layer returns its reason without waiting for the cap, and a
  sound one waits until it's defined. The badge test now waits for the graph to
  settle instead of for a timer.

❯ I am now seeing the error in a timely way, but note that this mechanism seems 
  redundant with the "possiblyRenderError" call in FluidView.js, see if you     
  can unify these. One scheme could be to look upwards for an {errorBoundary}   
  context from the site of the error which could hold a policy for rendering    
  the error, which would allow us e.g. to render the "Ask AI for help" badge -  
  note I am not seeing this badge currently. This approach might require all    
  widgets instantiated in the TLDraw canvas to be moved to be children of the   
  tlDrawHost itself so that behaviour appropriate to them can be scoped to it.  
  This would allow us to distinguish between user components in the canvas and  
  others such as those we'll implement to operate the integration itself. For   
  integration purposes they will still consider their own root path to be "/"   
  which will require translation when reading and writing PTF from them.

● The Othello paste now shows the "⚠ 1 problem" badge, already expanded, with
  the SFC conflict message and an "Ask the AI to fix this" button. I checked
  this in headless Chromium against the rebuilt demo. Pasting, playing a move,
  reloading and seeing the arena restored all work with widgets now living under
  the tldrawHost. All 1669 node tests pass and the browser demo script passes.

  Why you saw no badge: the conflicting layer was rejected, so the widget was
  never constructed. Without a component there was nothing to diagnose, so the
  host fell back to writing plain text into the shape.

  Unifying with possiblyRenderError
  - One route for errors: both places the framework rendered errors
    (possiblyRenderError, and failed text or class bindings in bindDomTokens)
    now call fluid.reportRenderError. It looks upwards for the nearest ancestor
    with the new fluid.errorBoundary layer and calls that ancestor's reportError
    method. With no boundary, the error is rendered in place exactly as before.
  - Upward walk, not a context lookup: tldrawHost is a fluid.resolveRoot
    component, so a {errorBoundary} context reference would resolve from
    anywhere, including the AI panel. Walking up through actual parents keeps
    the boundary scoped to its own descendants, which is the distinction you
    wanted.
  - Widgets whose own layer failed are now reported too, into the container
    their host supplied. Previously the framework rendered nothing for them.
  - A crash fix: fluid.renderError used to throw on an error with a single
    cause, which a rejected layer has.

  Widgets as children of the host
  - Framework: a new fluid.initChildComponent(parent, layerName, args) builds a
    component under a given parent. fluid.ptf.constructParts takes a matching
    parent option. Free components now go through the same code.
  - Construction: InfusionHost builds every widget inside tldrawHost. A widget
    whose layer has failed is built anyway, so its failure goes through the
    boundary like any other. The separate plain-text problem display is gone;
    everything now goes to the badge.
  - The boundary's role: tldrawHost is the boundary. Its policy
    (fluid.tldraw.reportWidgetError) finds the widget's host element and
    refreshes its badge instead of writing the error into the widget. Errors
    arising anywhere else are still rendered in place.

  Paths seen as /: PTF parts were already written relative to the widget.
  Diagnoses now are too, so the AI and the badge see paths like /view and
  {/view}.state.board wherever the widget actually sits. The {self} variant
  lists four missing-member problems plus the hint. I removed a "failed, but no
  cause was recorded" line that appeared alongside real causes.

  Limitations:
  - The framework only calls out to the boundary at the moment a component is
    set up, not continuously. The badge's live diagnosis still does the work of
    keeping it current (catching later breakage, recovery, and widgets that
    render nothing). The boundary routes errors to the badge and stops them
    being written into the widget.
  - Element ids now include the host's path, e.g.
    fl-id-tldrawHost-15.othello-26.
  - I only tested restore with a fresh arena. Arenas saved before this change
    should still restore, because saved records find widgets by shape id rather
    than by path, but I haven't tried one.

  New tests are in tests/framework-tests/core/js/FluidViewTests.js (boundary
  routing, and the in-place fallback) and
  tests/framework-tests/from-demos/js/TldrawAITests.js (a rejected widget under
  a boundary ending up on its badge, and path translation).

● Lint is clean. Now I'm rechecking the real demo in headless Chromium: the
  Othello badge, plus reload and restore.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● tldrawHost is no longer fluid.resolveRoot, and the error boundary is now found
  with ordinary context lookup. The Othello paste still shows the expanded "⚠ 1
  problem" badge with the SFC-conflict message, and a fixed Othello still keeps
  its moves across a reload. I checked both in headless Chromium against the
  rebuilt demo. npm test passes 1669/1669, the browser demo script passes, and
  eslint is clean.

  Framework (src/framework/core/js/FluidView.js)
  - Layer variety: fluid.errorBoundary changed from $variety: "framework" to
    "frameworkAux". Layers marked "framework" never have their names entered
    into scope, so {errorBoundary} could not have resolved. "frameworkAux"
    layers are entered into scope but still don't count as user layers, so the
    boundary stays out of the AI's layer listings and history.
  - Lookup: fluid.findErrorBoundary now resolves errorBoundary from the failing
    component's parent scope, the same scope chain fluid.resolveContext uses. It
    reads the scope record directly so it can return the boundary component
    itself. Starting from the parent means a failed boundary is never asked to
    report its own failure. If the nearest boundary is unavailable, the error is
    rendered in place rather than passed further up.
  - Sibling scope: under the standard rules, a boundary is also visible to its
    siblings' subtrees, not just its own descendants. That makes no difference
    to tldrawHost.

  Demo
  - fluid.demos.tldrawHost is now just $layers: "fluid.errorBoundary". Every
    widget, and each widget's view, is a child of it, so {tldrawHost}.editor,
    {tldrawHost}.notes and so on still resolve. The Ask-anything panel is
    outside its scope and never referred to it.
  - I updated the doc comment on tldrawHost, plus one comment in
    fluid-demos-storyImageGen.vue that described it as root-scoped.

  Tests: the stand-in host in
  tests/framework-tests/from-demos/js/TldrawAITests.js has also lost
  fluid.resolveRoot, to match the real one. The three composition tests now
  build their widgets under it with fluid.initChildComponent(host, …), and
  rebuild from PTF with {potentia, parent: host}. I also removed a my global
  from that file's header, which had become unused and failed eslint.

  The .mjs copies still haven't been regenerated.

❯ Let's cut down on the PTF noise by moving any of the genuinely variable       
  tldraw configuration onto the tldrawViewComponent itself, with sensible       
  defaults in the layer def for anything we need. We can then reconstruct       
  anything we need in the former "tldraw" layer from that information. It seems 
  that the "sequences" information is unnecessary since we're not expecting to  
  construct any shapes of those kinds, and I'm also not sure what role the      
  ptfToken plays since I don't see it correlated with anything else in the      
  document. Also, can we sort the layers consistently so that the most derived  
  layers in any hierarchy come first, with base layers at the end. The "live"   
  layer should come at the end of the document, as before. Therefore we expect  
  the first layer in the document to be the layer which is most definitive for  
  the definition of any widget which is being externalised, if there is just a  
  single one.

● A copied widget now carries no tldraw part at all. Its shape is described by a
  few lines in the root potentia, its own layer comes first, and the live part
  comes last. A plain hello-world copy is now just:

  layer fluid.demos.helloWorld
  potentia "/"  {"$layers": ["fluid.demos.helloWorld"],
                 "view": {"$component": {"shape": {"x": 200, "y": 200, "w": 260,
  "h": 80}}}}

  All 1685 node tests pass, the browser demo script passes, and eslint shows one
  error in tldraw-arena.js (encodeArenaRecord), in code I didn't touch. I also
  checked in headless Chromium:
  - Single widget: copy, delete, paste and copy again works, and the second copy
    matches the first.
  - Mixed copy: a note, a frame holding a rotated counter with live state, and
    an arrow bound to the counter all paste back intact. The same holds for an
    arena reload.
  - Old formats: an old-style copy (with schema, the widget inside the tldraw
    part, and a stale ptfToken) still pastes, and an old arena with separate
    shape parts still restores.
  - Othello: the pasted Othello still shows its badge, and the fixed Othello
    still keeps its moves across a reload.

  Shape configuration on fluid.tldrawViewComponent
  - New member: the layer now has a shape member whose defaults are rotation: 0,
    isLocked: false, opacity: 1, and null for x, y, w, h, parentId and index.
  - What's written: anything still at its default is left out. parentId is
    written only when the parent is another shape, such as a frame. index is
    written only when the document holds more than one shape, since its only job
    is ordering. A widget with no size gets its layer's preferred size, and one
    with no position goes where it is pasted.
  - Constructing: InfusionHost passes the shape's configuration in through
    hostPotentiaFor, which now takes it as a fourth argument. That puts the
    configuration on the view in the right place even when the view is
    cross-injected from a subcomponent.
  - Transmitting: when a widget is copied or saved,
    fluid.tldraw.writeShapeConfig rewrites that potentia from the shape as it is
    now. shapeId is dropped, because the receiving host supplies its own.
  - Not transmitted: the layer is now marked "frameworkAux", so it is no longer
    sent with every widget. That made the AI protocol's hostLayers exclusion
    list unnecessary, so I removed it.
  - Limitation: the live component's shape member records the shape as it was
    when the widget was built. It doesn't follow later moves or resizes; the
    current values are only read from the shape when the widget is copied or
    saved.

  The former "tldraw" part
  - Rebuilding shapes: partsToContent in tldraw-clipboard.js rebuilds each
    widget's tldraw shape from its configuration. Paste and arena restore both
    use it, and arena save uses the same contentToParts as copy, so the two
    formats are now the same.
  - Native shapes only: the tldraw part now appears only when native shapes are
    present. Their records are still tldraw's own full records, which are
    verbose; I left them as they are.
  - Dropped: schema (including sequences) and empty collections such as users
    are no longer written. You're right that sequences isn't needed: it's
    tldraw's migration version table, and on paste the editor now supplies its
    current schema. The trade-off is that content copied from an older tldraw
    version wouldn't be migrated.

  ptfToken: it was never meant to be in the document. It's an in-memory key: a
  pasted shape's meta.ptfToken tells InfusionHost, which only sees the shape
  record, where to find the parts waiting to build its widget. It leaked into
  documents because a pasted shape kept its meta and was later serialised as a
  tldraw record. Widget shapes are no longer serialised that way, so it can't
  appear any more.

  Layer order: fluid.ptf.sortLayerNames ranks each layer by the longest chain of
  derived layers above it, so base layers go last. Layers of equal rank keep
  the order they were found in. componentToParts finds the root component's own
  layers first, so a single widget's own layer comes first in the document. The
  workspace document sent to the AI is sorted the same way.

  AI workspace document: it keeps its separate page-space shape part, because
  the prompt describes that format and the AI replies in it. I strip the host's
  view potentia there instead of duplicating the shape.

  New tests are in tests/framework-tests/core/js/FluidPTFTests.js (layer order)
  and tests/framework-tests/from-demos/js/TldrawAITests.js (shape configuration,
  including the cross-injected case).

● Both widget fixes are in. The 1687 node tests, the browser demo script and
  lint all pass, and I checked the drag in headless Chromium.

  Show/hide feedback: the problem marker now has a disclosure arrow. It reads "⚠
  1 problem ▾" when the list is hidden and "⚠ 1 problem ▴" when it's open, and
  the marker turns a lighter red while open, so you get both of the cues you
  suggested. The button also reports its open or closed state to screen readers.
  The change is in fluid.tldraw.renderProblemBadge and the CSS in
  demo/tldraw/index.html.

  Dragging a widget in error: this was a real bug. InfusionHost swallowed
  pointer events anywhere inside the problem badge, including the open problem
  list. A widget that renders nothing shows that list straight away, and it
  covers most of the widget, so there was almost nothing left to grab or select.
  Now only real controls (the badge's buttons, plus the widget's own inputs and
  links) keep events from tldraw. The rest of the badge drags the shape like
  the widget's body does. In the browser, the same drag that used to leave the
  widget where it was now moves it 100px. The one thing lost is selecting the
  problem text with the mouse; "Ask the AI to fix this" still passes it on.

  Floating on top: I couldn't reproduce this part. In my test the broken widget
  and a hello-world widget stacked by tldraw's ordinary z-order whichever came
  first, and "send to back" worked. The badge's own layering is confined to its
  shape. My guess is that what you saw was the drag bug: a freshly pasted widget
  is always the topmost shape, and because it couldn't be grabbed or selected,
  it couldn't be moved or sent back. If it still floats on top after you
  rebuild, tell me the steps and I'll look again.

  Chat history: yes, the protocol sends recent conversation history with each
  request, but only part of it:
  - What each request holds: a fresh system prompt (docs, the current workspace
    as PTF, and your selection), then the last 8 earlier messages
    (fluid.tldraw.ai.maxHistory), then your new direction.
  - What's in the history: your earlier directions, the AI's replies kept
    exactly as sent (including any reasoning and any PTF documents), and the
    "your response could not be applied" messages from repair rounds. The
    workspace isn't in the history, because it's always sent fresh in the system
    prompt.
  - Within one exchange: every repair round resends the whole exchange so far.
  - Where it lives: in memory only, as fluid.demos.askAnything.history.
    Reloading the page starts an empty conversation. What the panel displays is
    kept separately and isn't what gets sent.

  Two quirks in how the history is cut down are worth knowing:
  - Messages, not exchanges: the 8 is counted in messages. One exchange that
    needed two repairs uses 6 messages, so it can push nearly everything else
    out.
  - Cut mid-exchange: the 8 can start in the middle of an exchange, so the
    history may open with an AI reply or a repair message whose request has been
    dropped.

  Trimming to whole exchanges instead would fix both. Want me to make that
  change?

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● The history now only ever sends whole exchanges. All 1692 node tests pass,
  eslint is clean, and the demo is rebuilt. I didn't try it against a live
  model.

  - Stored as exchanges: fluid.tldraw.ai.converse now keeps the history as a
    list of exchanges rather than one flat list of messages. An exchange is your
    direction, the AI's replies, and any problem reports sent back for repair.
    The messages are still sent exactly as before, with no extra fields.
  - Trimming: the new fluid.tldraw.ai.recentExchanges walks back from the newest
    exchange and keeps whole exchanges while their total stays within
    maxHistory (still 8 messages).
  - Where it stops: the first exchange that doesn't fit stops the walk, so the
    exchanges sent are always consecutive. A small older exchange can't be
    slipped in behind a large one that was dropped.
  - Large latest exchange: if the most recent exchange alone were over the
    limit, no history would be sent. That can't happen at the current settings:
    with 2 repairs allowed, an exchange is at most 6 messages.

  The panel's own code (fluid-demos-askAnything.vue) only passes the history
  through, so only its comment changed. The IFM harness in a-scratch starts with
  an empty history and ignores what comes back, so it's unaffected.

  The existing tests in TldrawAITests.js now check the history's new shape. A
  new set of cases covers trimming:
  - an exchange that would go over the limit is dropped whole, along with
    everything before it;
  - exchanges are sent whole up to the limit;
  - nothing is sent when the latest exchange alone is too large;
  - the built message list matches the selection.

❯ When a PTF response comes back to the AI, before writing it to the workspace, 
  for any layers in it that are not already present, add a <provenance> record  
  in their SFC with fields recording <model> the id of the model which          
  generated it, <prompt> the user's prompt which generated it and <datestamp>   
  the ISO date, make sure that the SFC reading and writing machinery will pass  
  through this new block

      710      /**
      711 +     * The provenance of a Single File Component, recorded in 
          +a top-level `<provenance>` block holding an element for       
      712 +     * each of its fields, e.g. by an AI integration recording
          + how the layer it defines was generated. The block takes      
      713 +     * no part in defining the layer, and is carried through v
          +erbatim with the rest of the SFC's text.                      
      714 +     * @typedef {Object} SFCProvenance                        
      715 +     * @property {String} [model] - The id of the AI model whi
          +ch generated the SFC                                          
      716 +     * @property {String} [prompt] - The user's prompt from wh
          +ich it was generated                                          
      717 +     * @property {String} [datestamp] - The ISO date at which 
          +it was generated                                              
      718 +     */                                                       
      719 +                                                              
      720 +    // The fields of an SFC's provenance, in the order in whic
          +h they are written                                            
      721 +    fluid.sfcProvenanceFields = ["model", "prompt", "datestamp
          +"];                                                           
      722 +                                                              
      723 +    /**                                                       
      724 +     * Escape text for inclusion as the text content of an HTM
          +L element                                                     
      725 +     * @param {String} text - The text to be escaped          
      726 +     * @return {String} The escaped text                      
      727 +     */                                                       
      728 +    fluid.encodeHtmlText = text => text.replace(/&/g, "&amp;")
          +.replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/>/g, "&gt;");                 
      729 +                                                              
      730 +    /**                                                       
      731 +     * Find the top-level `<provenance>` blocks of a Single Fi
          +le Component                                                  
      732 +     * @param {VNode} vTree - The virtual DOM tree representin
          +g the Single File Component                                   
      733 +     * @return {VNode[]} The blocks, in document order        
      734 +     */                                                       
      735 +    fluid.sfcProvenanceNodes = vTree => vTree.children.filter(
          +child => child.tag === "provenance");                         
      736 +                                                              
      737 +    /**                                                       
      738 +     * Read the provenance recorded in the text of a Single Fi
          +le Component                                                  
      739 +     * @param {String} text - The text of the SFC             
      740 +     * @return {SFCProvenance|null} The provenance recorded in
          + its first `<provenance>` block, or `null` if it has none     
      741 +     */                                                       
      742 +    fluid.readSFCProvenance = function (text) {               
      743 +        const node = fluid.sfcProvenanceNodes(fluid.parseHTMLT
          +oTree(text))[0];                                              
      744 +        let togo = null;                                      
      745 +        if (node) {                                           
      746 +            togo = {};                                        
      747 +            node.children.forEach(child => {                  
      748 +                if (fluid.sfcProvenanceFields.includes(child.t
          +ag)) {                                                        
      749 +                    togo[child.tag] = child.children.map(textN
          +ode => textNode.text ?? "").join("");                         
      750 +                }                                             
      751 +            });                                               
      752 +        }                                                     
      753 +        return togo;                                          
      754 +    };                                                        
      755 +                                                              
      756 +    /**                                                       
      757 +     * Write provenance into the text of a Single File Compone
          +nt, as a `<provenance>` block at its end replacing any        
      758 +     * which it already holds. Fields whose values are absent 
          +are omitted from the block.                                   
      759 +     * @param {String} text - The text of the SFC             
      760 +     * @param {SFCProvenance} provenance - The provenance to b
          +e written                                                     
      761 +     * @return {String} The text of the SFC holding the proven
          +ance                                                          
      762 +     */                                                       
      763 +    fluid.writeSFCProvenance = function (text, provenance) {  
      764 +        const existing = fluid.sfcProvenanceNodes(fluid.parseH
          +TMLToTree(text));                                             
      765 +        // Remove existing blocks from the last, so that the o
          +ffsets of earlier ones remain valid                           
      766 +        const stripped = existing.reduceRight((acc, node) => a
          +cc.slice(0, node.start) + acc.slice(node.end), text);         
      767 +        const fields = fluid.sfcProvenanceFields.filter(field 
          +=> typeof(provenance[field]) === "string")                    
      768 +            .map(field => `    <${field}>${fluid.encodeHtmlTex
          +t(provenance[field])}</${field}>\n`);                         
      769 +        return stripped.trimEnd() + "\n\n<provenance>\n" + fie
          +lds.join("") + "</provenance>\n";                             
      770 +    };                                                        
      771 +

      775       * the layer definition with appropriate metadata. It also
            handles template blocks and style nodes.

       964 +/**                                                          
       965 + * Record the provenance of an SFC layer part sent by the AI.
           + A layer which is not yet defined is stamped with the        
       966 + * provenance of the response defining it, replacing any `<pr
           +ovenance>` block the AI wrote itself. A layer which is       
       967 + * already defined keeps the provenance recorded in its curre
           +nt SFC, if any, whatever the AI sent - so that a layer       
       968 + * resent with changes, e.g. in correcting a response, is sti
           +ll attributed to the exchange which created it. Other        
       969 + * parts are returned unchanged.                             
       970 + * @param {PTFPart} part - The layer part                    
       971 + * @param {SFCProvenance} provenance - The provenance of the 
           +response holding the part                                    
       972 + * @param {Function} isDefined - Accepts a layer name and ret
           +urns `true` if the layer is currently defined                
       973 + * @return {PTFPart} The part, with its provenance recorded  
       974 + */                                                          
       975 +fluid.tldraw.ai.stampLayerPart = function (part, provenance, 
           +isDefined) {                                                 
       976 +    let togo = part;                                         
       977 +    if (part.kind === "layer" && part.contentType === fluid.l
           +ayerMimeTypes.sfc) {                                         
       978 +        try {                                                
       979 +            if (!isDefined(part.filename)) {                 
       980 +                togo = {...part, body: fluid.writeSFCProvenan
           +ce(part.body, provenance)};                                  
       981 +            } else {                                         
       982 +                const currentText = fluid.sfcStore[part.filen
           +ame]?.textSignal.get();                                      
       983 +                const current = typeof(currentText) === "stri
           +ng" ? fluid.readSFCProvenance(currentText) : null;           
       984 +                if (current) {                               
       985 +                    togo = {...part, body: fluid.writeSFCProv
           +enance(part.body, current)};                                 
       986 +                }                                            
       987 +            }                                                
       988 +        } catch (e) {                                        
       989 +            // Markup which cannot be parsed is reported when
           + the layer is registered                                     
       990 +            togo = part;                                     
       991 +        }                                                    
       992 +    }                                                        
       993 +    return togo;                                             
       994 +};                                                           
       995 +                                                             
       996 +/**                                                          
       997 + * Record the provenance of every SFC layer part of the chang
           +es planned from a response of the AI, at the top level       
       998 + * of its document and within its component parts - see `flui
           +d.tldraw.ai.stampLayerPart`                                  
       999 + * @param {ChangePlan} plan - The planned changes, whose laye
           +r parts are replaced by ones recording their provenance      
      1000 + * @param {SFCProvenance} provenance - The provenance of the 
           +response                                                     
      1001 + * @param {Function} [isDefined] - Accepts a layer name and r
           +eturns `true` if the layer is currently defined,             
      1002 + * defaulting to `fluid.ptf.isLayerDefined`                  
      1003 + */                                                          
      1004 +fluid.tldraw.ai.stampProvenance = function (plan, provenance,
           + isDefined = fluid.ptf.isLayerDefined) {                     
      1005 +    const stamp = part => fluid.tldraw.ai.stampLayerPart(part
           +, provenance, isDefined);                                    
      1006 +    plan.layers = plan.layers.map(stamp);                    
      1007 +    [...plan.creations, ...plan.updates].forEach(change => { 
      1008 +        change.parts = change.parts.map(stamp);              
      1009 +    });                                                      
      1010 +};

● Both suites pass. Now running the full suite, lint and build.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● When the AI sends back new SFC layers, each one now gets a <provenance> block
  before it's written to the workspace. I haven't run it against a live model
  yet; all 1713 node tests and the 20 browser tests pass, lint is clean and the
  demo is rebuilt.

  The block is added at the end of the SFC. Characters like < and & in the
  prompt are escaped so they can't be mistaken for markup:
  <provenance>
      <model>…</model>
      <prompt>…</prompt>
      <datestamp>2026-10-23T12:00:00.000Z</datestamp>
  </provenance>

  Framework (FluidView.js, FluidPTF.js)
  - The SFC loader only looks at <script>, <template> and <style>, and SFC text
    is stored and sent in PTF exactly as written. So the block already passed
    through untouched; the new test confirms it.
  - fluid.readSFCProvenance(text) and fluid.writeSFCProvenance(text, provenance)
    read and write the block. Writing replaces any existing block and leaves
    out fields with no value.
  - fluid.ptf.isLayerDefined(layerName) checks whether a layer is already
    present, taken from the existing check in registerLayerPartQuietly, which
    now uses it.

  AI side (ai-protocol.js)
  - converse now takes a model option. The panel and the IFM harness both pass
    it.
  - After the response is planned and before it is applied,
    fluid.tldraw.ai.stampProvenance stamps each new SFC layer in the plan,
    whether top-level or nested in a component part. It records the model, your
    direction as the prompt, and the current time.
  - If the AI wrote a provenance block of its own in a new layer, it's replaced.
  - The system prompt now tells the AI that these blocks are maintained by the
    workspace: it should never write one and should leave existing ones
    unchanged.

  Choices to check
  - Layers that already exist keep their current provenance, even if the AI
    drops or changes the block when resending. This also covers repair rounds: a
    layer defined in the first attempt is already present when it's resent, so
    it stays credited to the original prompt. Existing layers with no
    provenance, such as hand-written ones, are left as sent.
  - Only SFC layers are stamped. JavaScript layers have nowhere to hold the
    block.
  - The datestamp is a full ISO timestamp, not just the date.
  - The chat's "change" entry still shows the AI's document as it arrived,
    without the block.

  Tests
  - FluidPTFTests.js: writing and reading the block, an SFC with a block loading
    and rendering, and its text being sent verbatim.
  - TldrawAITests.js: new layers stamped, forged blocks replaced, existing
    provenance kept, hand-written and JavaScript layers untouched, nested layers
    stamped, and an end-to-end exchange recording the model and direction.

● Checking the shape of parseResponse's result for the console snippet:

● Your saved PTF has lost the othello functions because PTF never kept the text
  of a JavaScript layer. When it sends one, it rewrites the layer from its
  fluid.def(...) definition. Anything else the AI wrote alongside, like
  fluid.demos.othello.legalMoves = function ..., isn't part of that definition,
  so it's dropped.

  I've fixed that so the AI's text is kept, and JavaScript layers now carry the
  same provenance record as comments at the end. All 1725 node tests and 20
  browser tests pass, lint is clean and the demo is rebuilt. I haven't run the
  console snippet below on your page.

  How it happened

  - Why it still plays: when a JavaScript layer part arrives,
    fluid.ptf.registerLayerPart evaluates its text. That ran the fluid.def call
    and assigned the functions, which is why they're in your globals and the
    widget works.
  - Why it's lost on the way out: fluid.ptf.layerToPart had no stored text to go
    back to (SFCs have fluid.sfcStore, JavaScript layers had nothing), so it
    rewrote the layer from its definition. Only the fluid.def call came back.
  - Where else it bites: the same rewriting happens when the arena is saved to
    localStorage, in the undo history, and in the workspace the AI is shown each
    turn. If you reload the page as it stands, the restored othello widget will
    fail, because the saved layer has no functions.
  - Why the AI sent JavaScript at all: the system prompt allows it
    ("text/javascript for a fluid.def(...) call"). With a computed template
    there's no <template> block, so it had no particular reason to choose an
    SFC.
  - The two failed attempts (my guess): they look like ordering mistakes in the
    AI's text. "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'initialBoard')"
    fits a definition that calls fluid.demos.othello.initialBoard() before
    fluid.def has created fluid.demos.othello. "Cannot set … (setting
    'initialBoard')" fits assigning to it before that point.

  What I changed

  - Text kept (FluidPTF.js): a new fluid.ptf.jsLayerSources records a JavaScript
    layer part's text when it is registered, before it is evaluated, so the
    undo history captures it too. layerToPart sends that text as long as the
    layer still has the definition it produced. If the layer is redefined some
    other way, such as a direct fluid.def call, it falls back to rewriting it
    from the definition. This also fixes arena saving and undo/redo.
  - Provenance in comments:
    - fluid.writeJSProvenance and fluid.readJSProvenance (in FluidView.js) write
      and read the same <provenance> block as line comments at the end of the
      text.
    - fluid.ptf.readLayerProvenance and fluid.ptf.writeLayerProvenance pick the
      SFC or JavaScript form by the part's type.
    - stampLayerPart now uses them, so new JavaScript layers get stamped and
      existing ones keep their record.

    The comment block looks like this:
  // <provenance>
  //     <model>…</model>
  //     <prompt>Write a widget that lets me play Othello</prompt>
  //     <datestamp>2026-10-23T…Z</datestamp>
  // </provenance>
  - System prompt:
    - It now says a layer's text is kept as sent, functions included.
    - It tells the AI to assign those functions after the fluid.def call and
      never call them while writing the definition. That should avoid failures
      like your first two attempts.
    - The provenance note now covers both kinds of layer.
  - Tests:
    - JavaScript text survives sending, the component's parts and the history
      source.
    - It falls back to rewriting after a direct fluid.def.
    - Comment provenance round-trips.
    - New JavaScript layers sent by the AI get stamped.

  Rescuing your Othello layer now

  Run this in the page that's still open, before reloading. Your current page
  doesn't have the fix, and a reload would bring back the version without
  functions.

  The othello layer is JavaScript, so it has no record in fluid.sfcStore to
  patch. Instead, the snippet re-registers the AI's original text as an SFC (in
  a <script> block) with a <provenance> block, which creates that record. The
  arena will then save it, and it will survive a reload with or without the fix.

  It takes the text from the AI's last reply, still held in
  fluid.demos.askAnything.history. If that can't be found, it rebuilds it from
  the definition plus the functions now in your globals. The datestamp is the
  time you run it, since the actual response time isn't recorded anywhere.

  (async () => {
      const layerName = "fluid.demos.othello";
      const prompt = "Write a widget that lets me play Othello";
      const findLayer = parts => parts.flatMap(part => part.parts ?
  findLayer(part.parts) : [part])
          .find(part => part.kind === "layer" && part.filename === layerName);
      const exchange = fluid.demos.askAnything.history.at(-1) || [];
      const reply = exchange.filter(message => message.role ===
  "assistant").at(-1);
      const response = reply ? fluid.tldraw.ai.parseResponse(reply.content) :
  null;
      let body = response?.type === "ptf" ? findLayer(response.parts)?.body :
  undefined;
      let source = "the AI's final reply";
      if (body === undefined) {
          const namespace = fluid.getGlobalValue(layerName);
          const functions = Object.entries(namespace).filter(([, value]) =>
  typeof(value) === "function")
              .map(([key, value]) => `${layerName}.${key} =
  ${value.toString()};`);
          body = fluid.ptf.layerToPart(layerName).body + "\n\n" +
  functions.join("\n\n") + "\n";
          source = "the current definition and the functions in its namespace";
      }
      const model = [...document.querySelectorAll(".ask-anything-model select, 
  .ask-anything-model input")]
          .find(element =>
  !element.classList.contains("ask-anything-hidden"))?.value;
      const escape = text => text.replace(/&/g, "&amp;").replace(/</g,
  "&lt;").replace(/>/g, "&gt;");
      const fields = Object.entries({model, prompt, datestamp: new
  Date().toISOString()})
          .filter(([, value]) => typeof(value) === "string")
          .map(([key, value]) => `    
  <${key}>${escape(value)}</${key}>\n`).join("");
      if (body.includes("</script")) {
          console.error("The layer's text holds </script and cannot be wrapped 
  in an SFC");
      } else {
          const sfc = `<script>\n${body.trim()}\n</script>\n\n<provenance>\n${fi
  elds}</provenance>\n`;
          fluid.ptf.registerLayerPart({kind: "layer", contentType: "text/x-vue",
  filename: layerName, body: sfc});
          await fluid.cell.waitFor(fluid.readSFC(layerName).docInjectEffects.get
  (document).injDone);
          await fluid.cell.settle();
          const stored = fluid.ptf.layerToPart(layerName);
          console.log(`Re-registered ${layerName} as an SFC from ${source} - now
  ${stored.contentType}, `
              + `functions retained: ${stored.body.includes(".legalMoves")}`);
          console.log(stored.body);
      }
  })();

  - What to check after running: it should log text/x-vue and functions 
    retained: true. The widget updates in place.
  - What the swap adds: an undo step in the layer history.
  - Afterwards: export the widget again or let the arena save. The PTF will then
    hold the full text and its provenance.
